# Fidelity register

Generated by `tools/fidelity_register.py`; edit the source annotations or declarations, then regenerate.

The acceptance gate is **zero unresolved assumptions in required original behavior**. All APPROX IDs below remain unresolved while their labels exist. Tags describing developer-only behavior or presentation conventions also remain listed; this inventory does not judge their relevance or downgrade them.

EXT labels identify deliberate extensions. DATA labels identify claimed original-data provenance. BIN labels cite the original executable logic a rule was traced to (`STP-PPC` = Mac PowerPC Sim Theme Park, `TPI-EXE` = Theme Park Inc Game.exe, `TPW-EXE` = Windows Sim Theme Park World v2.0 tp.exe; see docs/reverse/FINDINGS.md). None of these labels proves semantic correctness or cancels an APPROX label at the same site. Acceptance also requires evidence for required behavior, assets, inputs and outputs; a zero tag count alone cannot satisfy that gate.

This is a tag inventory, not enforcement of all semantics. It does not detect unlabelled magic numbers, formulas, logic, omitted features or false provenance claims. It scans whole-line C# // and /// comments under source, excluding bin/obj; inline trailing comments, block comments and other languages are outside its scope. ID templates and runtime log string templates are not code-site annotations.

```sh
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --write
python3 tools/fidelity_register.py --check
python3 -m unittest discover -s tools -p 'test_fidelity_register.py' -v
```

Current inventory: **396 unresolved unique APPROX IDs**, 483 APPROX occurrences, 207 EXT occurrences, 194 DATA occurrences and 991 BIN occurrences.

Of these, 372 IDs belong to the existing original-fidelity areas (required-behavior scope remains unadjudicated), and 24 ONLINE IDs record uncertainty inside the allowed OpenTPW online extension. The extension scope does not hide, resolve or subtract those APPROX IDs from the total.

CI checks annotation/declaration consistency and document freshness only. It does not fail the build based on the unresolved count and does not establish the original-fidelity release gate.

Repeated source occurrences of one ID are allowed and listed separately. Duplicate register declarations, malformed/missing ID syntax, unregistered IDs and declarations without source annotations fail the check. Register declarations are read from the 24 configured C# registers (current one-entry-per-line tuple/new syntax) and the explicit three-column Approximation register table in docs/ONLINE.md; these are bounded lexical readers, not general C# or Markdown parsers. New registers must be explicitly configured. The check also compares the complete regenerated document against this file.

## Retired and reserved IDs

IDs that a table row in docs keys, or docs prose cites as `APPROX:ID`, but no register declares: retired approximations and IDs a plan reserves. Declaring one of them fails the check, so a new approximation cannot take a number the docs already use with another meaning. `--write` keeps every listed ID; remove an entry by hand only when its docs agree with the new declaration.

| VM-901 | `source/OpenTPW/VM/Handlers/Scheduling.cs:24` | [APPROX:VM-901] The traced facts are STP-PPC 0xb0710–0xb0798: WAITABS stores now + operand as a 32-bit deadline word in the field WAIT uses (no script-speed division), its first encounter always yields, and later encounters wait while unsigned now &lt; deadline. The port models that deadline in its non-wrapping double script clock, so the original's unsigned comparison, its 2^32 wrap and its zero-deadline re-arm sentinel (docs/reverse/PPC-clock.md) are not modelled; a session that never nears 2^31 ms of scheduler clock cannot observe the difference. |
| VM-901 | `source/OpenTPW/VM/RideVM.cs:459` | [APPROX:VM-901] The deadline lives in this VM's non-wrapping double script clock, not the original's wrapping unsigned 32-bit word with its zero-deadline sentinel (docs/reverse/PPC-clock.md); wrap-past-2^31/2^32 behaviour is unmodelled. |

| WALK-004 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestNavigation.cs:182` | [APPROX:WALK-004] routes are OpenTPW's cell sequences (one path cell per destination, the queue cells in order) refilled five at a time and rerouted by prepending the current cell to the rest of the route; the original's search 0x101c64 and its refill are not traced beyond the route-store points — evidence needed: 0x101c64 and the +164/+168 bookkeeping |
| WALK-005 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestSimulation.cs:680` | [APPROX:WALK-005] a cell is walkable when it is on the current walk (its route cells and the walk's extra open cells), so path cells beside the route are walls; the original tests the cell's edge flags against the guest's +180 mask (0xd768c) and commits the step through a transition call (0xfee3c..0xfee58) — evidence needed: the edge flags 0xd768c reads, the +180 mask per guest state and the commit callback |
| WALK-006 | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestSimulation.cs:1405` | [APPROX:WALK-006] a queue walk may also enter the queue cell ahead of its destination and the one behind its start (the entrance and the join cell at the ends), and no other cell off its route; this stands in for the original's edge flags along the queue — evidence needed: the queue cells' edge flags and the +180 mask in states 12 and 13 |

| TPW-EXE:0x004DE840 queue slot | `source/OpenTPW/World/Guests/GuestSimulation.cs:1435` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x004DE840 queue slot] depth = trunc(255 × 0.25 × the place within the cell); lateral = 0x72 + rand % 28. Up to depth 128 the cell's own link to the front picks the axis: link 1 (−Y) gives (lateral, depth), 4 (+X) (255 − depth, lateral), 16 (+Y) (lateral, 255 − depth), 64 (−X) (depth, lateral). Beyond 128 (the fourth place) the axis is the link of the queue cell behind (0x004DE670), for the back cell the direction from its attached path cell (the first of its connections 1, 4, 16, 64 that leads to a path cell, reversed), and link 4 then gives depth − 128 instead of 255 − depth |

| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon heights | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:62` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon heights] h0 = the track dummy's Y with the loft clip at clamp(fLoftAnimAdd, StartCap, EndCap) (0x430923..0x430990), h1 = its Y at clamp(fLoftAnimMultiplier + fLoftAnimAdd, StartCap, EndCap) (0x430a0b..0x430a92), the stack offset the stack dummy's Y at the first pose minus h0, or 0 without a stack dummy (0x4309be..0x430a01) |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon model tags | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:15` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon model tags] the loft, rotate, incline and bank clips and the track and stack dummies are looked up on the loaded model by node attribute (0x2, 0x3, 0x9, 0xa, 0x1000000, 0x800000, 0x2000000, 0x100000, 0x80000) when the control is set up (0x42eb70) |
| TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon track dummy | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterPylonRig.cs:56` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x430590 pylon track dummy] the track dummy is the dummy numbered by the first attribute record with bit 0x800000 (0x4306be..0x4306e9: record index +0x2c, dummy number − 1 at +0x30), the stack dummy the one of the first record with 0x100000 (0x430780..0x4307a6); the numbers count the model's dummy nodes from 1 in file order; a model without such a record falls back to the first dummy named Track* or animated by the loft clip |
| TPW-EXE:0x4309a4 pylon heights | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterDefinition.cs:258` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4309a4 pylon heights] pylon +0xbc = h0, +0xc0 = h1, +0x68 (fMinHeight) = max(fMinHeight, h0) (0x4309a4..0x4309bb), +0xc4 = stack dummy Y − h0 or 0 |
| TPW-EXE:0x4310c8 auto-bank | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:714` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4310c8 auto-bank] a node banks unless it carries node flag 0x4 (or 0x100 forces bank 0); the PC build path never sets 0x4 and the station end node's 0x144 are creation parameters (0x434060), so the end node banks like any pylon |
| TPW-EXE:0x433280 stacked height | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:603` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x433280 stacked height] the height of a stacked node is the sum over the nodes below it of (h0 + t(h1 − h0) + stack offset) of the node's own pylon, then its own h0 + t(h1 − h0): each level adds the pylon's stack offset (+0xc4) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:449` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434060 node creation parameters] the constructor maps its parameter bits to node flags: 2 → 0x2000, 8 → 0x200, 0x10 → 0x400, 0x20 → 0x1800, 0x40 → 0x80000, 0x80 → 0x40000, 0x100 → 0x100000 (0x4343a6..0x4343eb), bit 0 clear → 0x200000 on the node and its predecessor, bit 4 goes to 0x433bd0; the end node's 0x144 is therefore 0x80000 \| 0x100000, no loop flag 0x40, no 0x4 and no 0x100. The Mac call site 0x10037E34 passes the same 0x144; its node constructor could not be compared (no Mac binary here) |
| TPW-EXE:0x434153 loop pair | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:375` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x434153 loop pair] a node whose pylon control has flag 0x4000 gets node flags \|0xe0 and loop rotate (+0xf8) 0 when it is the first of the pair, \|0x60 and 0.5 when it is the second (the pair counter 0x79c664 is 2, then 1); a trough pylon (0x8000) sets neither |
| TPW-EXE:0x4349a5 distance waiver | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:215` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x4349a5 distance waiver] a link outside [min², max²] is rejected when the previous node's ordinal ([ebp]) is 2 and otherwise waived when its descriptor has flag 2 (0x4349af..0x4349b9): the waiver exempts the station's internal nodes (ordinals 0 and 1, created by 0x436ac0); OpenTPW's one station node has no such predecessor, so every link it builds is checked as in the original (COAST-007 retired; the three-node station is COAST-029) |
| TPW-EXE:0x435020 place debit | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterBuilder.cs:108` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435020 place debit] the PC coaster click path (0x524960 → 0x4419b0 → 0x435020) reads the balance (0x6ad810) and ignores it, then debits ftol(cost) through 0x4d01f0 when money is on (0x785a2c == 0): there is no balance comparison on the path, so nothing here refuses a pylon for money |
| TPW-EXE:0x435944 mode setter | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:527` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435944 mode setter] from mode 64 to 16 or 32 the power factor becomes 1 and every train gets flag 0x80 and loses 0x40 (0x435947..0x435950); entering mode 64 sets the power factor to 0 (0x4359d5) |
| TPW-EXE:0x435ac9 controller tick | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:699` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435ac9 controller tick] the motion tick runs when state & 0xE (2 built, 4 open, 8 closed); the station step (0x43b8b0, then 0x439570) only when state & 0xC and mode is not 0x40 (0x435cd1, 0x435cd5); the motion tick has no zero-speed branch, so a train in the station keeps the winch floor's speed |
| TPW-EXE:0x435adf scroll shift | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRenderer.cs:24` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435adf scroll shift] the per-frame tick shifts V by δ = (now − ctl+0x138)·P·0.001 for every polygon of a texture the loader flagged (definition flag 4, set when any fScrollRate is nonzero; texture flag 0x20), P the power factor at controller +0x2c; the stored fScrollRate (texture +0xc) is not read in that loop |
| TPW-EXE:0x435d50 build cursor | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:769` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x435d50 build cursor] every frame the edited coaster (data 0x790fec, flag 4) runs this edit step: for a hovered cell within 300 units of the last node it fills the coaster's spare cursor node (controller +0x68, 0x436190, 0x436230) with the cell and links it after the last node, then rebuilds the track (0x444700) — the cursor node is a regular node of the chain, so the curve is drawn through it before the pylon is placed; a hovered cell that already holds a node of the chain marks the chain's start as closing (flag 0x8) instead |
| TPW-EXE:0x436dd4 train constructor | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:342` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x436dd4 train constructor] a new train has position 0 and speed 0 (train +0x74/+0x78), whatever its place; the tick accelerates it with the floors and gravity like any other |
| TPW-EXE:0x439920 link boxes | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:61` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439920 link boxes] every node with a successor gets the box of the samples from its own index to its successor's, grown by the radius of its pylon control (its successor's without one, 0 without either) |
| TPW-EXE:0x439ab0 intersection pass | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:45` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439ab0 intersection pass] a node marked 0x200000 (and not 0x10000) first drops its entries (0x43ad50), clears 0x600000, then tests its link against the links starting two nodes after it, and then those starting two nodes before it, with 0x439c40; the pass runs with the rounding mode set to truncate (0x439ac4), so every sine-table index below truncates |
| TPW-EXE:0x439ab0 track collision | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterTrack.cs:261` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439ab0 track collision] the original tests the track against itself only (CoasterCollision, run on every rebuild): no terrain or scenery query exists in 0x439ab0, 0x439c40 or 0x43a2b0, so a pylon placement is never refused for what stands under or beside the track |
| TPW-EXE:0x439c87 pylon-less skip | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:118` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439c87 pylon-less skip] a link that ends on a pylon-less node (the end node) is not tested against the link that leaves the first node, nor the reverse (the end node stands where the first node does) |
| TPW-EXE:0x439cc6 box overlap | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:144` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439cc6 box overlap] all six strict comparisons A.min &lt; C.max and C.min &lt; A.max |
| TPW-EXE:0x439f35 segment overlap | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:158` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x439f35 segment overlap] the segment boxes overlap on all three axes (strict) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43a33b pose points | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:201` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43a33b pose points] roll: x' = x·cos − y·sin, y' = x·sin + y·cos; pitch: y' = y·cos − z·sin, z' = y·sin + z·cos; yaw: x' = x·cos + z·sin, z' = z·cos − x·sin; the sin index is fistp(f32(angle · scale)) & mask and the cos index fistp(f32(scale · f32(angle − (−π/2)))) & mask, truncating (the pass sets the control word 0xC00); sample +0x10 yaw, +0x14 pitch, +0x18 roll |
| TPW-EXE:0x43a5f4 face plane | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:228` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43a5f4 face plane] n = normalise((P1 − P0) × (P1 − P2)), d = −(P1 · n); t = −(n · B + d) / (n · e) for the edge B → B + e, accepted for 0 ≤ t ≤ 1 (0x43a718, 0x43a8d0) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43a8b3 face test | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:229` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43a8b3 face test] the corners P0, P1, P2, P3 give unit vectors u from the cut point; the sum acos(u0·u1) + acos(u1·u2) + acos(u2·u3) + acos(u3·u0) must not be below 6.2821855; the same pairs, in the same order, are summed by the longitudinal edge's test 0x43aa10 |
| TPW-EXE:0x43ad50 drop entries | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterCollision.cs:85` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43ad50 drop entries] a node without overflow removes itself from the list of every partner in its own list; a node with overflow from every node's list; the count falls by every entry removed and by the node's own entries |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b0e0 coaster admission | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:96` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b0e0 coaster admission] COAST 1 pushes the guest on the admission ring (controller +0xb0); a train in the station seats the guests from it (0x43b600) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b0e0 coaster boarding | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:503` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b0e0 coaster boarding] COAST 1 pushes the guest on the ring at ctl+0xb0 when its count is below the capacity (definition +0x2f8, a queue size OpenTPW does not read; the room above bounds it); nobody is seated here |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b1f0 coaster room | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:495` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b1f0 coaster room] COAST 2 returns max(0, ctl+0xb8 − ctl+0xc0 − ctl+0xbc): the capacity COAST 6 stored (0x43bbd1: ctl+0xf0 → +0xb8), minus the ring's second counter (+0xc0, only ever 0), minus the guests on the admission ring (+0xbc); no state, mode or train enters it, so the room is the capacity of one train whether or not a train is in the station |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b2b0 coaster speed setter | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:472` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b2b0 coaster speed setter] the loop 0x5516b0 calls it every pass with the signed 16-bit script bias at script +0xC0 (50 at creation, 0x558c3c); applied via 0x43c3d0 (setting +0x30, cap +0x34 = fmadds-style lerp of definition +0x4C and +0x50 by s·0.01, dirty flags 0x1001, the per-node pass 0x43afa0 on flag 8) when it differs from the setting and money is off (0x785a2c set) or controller +0 bit 0x8000 is clear; bit 0x8000 and the money switch are not modelled (treated as clear), and since the script's bias (RideVM.SpeedBias, the ride's InitSpeed) is only written at ride init (RIDES-033) the first pass after COAST 8 is the only one that changes anything |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b2f0 coaster close | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:443` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b2f0 coaster close] gated: argument 0 opens (state → 4) only when state & 0xA (closed 8 or incomplete 2), a nonzero argument closes (→ 8) only when state & 4 (open), like the Mac; the bool Closed is state 8/2 versus 4, so each branch changes nothing when already there. Closing (4 → 8, 0x43b390) moves the admission ring (+0xB0) into the leaving ring (+0xD0) that COAST 3 (0x43b220) pops; OpenTPW has no admission ring (a guest is seated at once, see Board), so nothing waits to be released |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b330 coaster capacity | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:457` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b330 coaster capacity] min(max(request, 0), definition +0x318 (the fleet's seat socket total), the first train definition's +8); the lower bound is the literal 0 where the Mac reading takes a global (documented difference); a changed value is rebuilt through 0x43ba90 (seat allocation, not modelled) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b390 coaster close | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:519` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b390 coaster close] moves min(leaving ring room, admission count) guests from the admission ring (+0xb0) to the leaving ring (+0xd0), oldest first; the leaving ring's size (2 × definition +0x318) is not modelled, so every waiting guest moves |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b600 seat guests | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:662` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b600 seat guests] train +0x14 = the free seats of the span's cars (car +0x44 − car +0x34), counted by the caller; each guest popped from the ring (+0xc4) draws s = s·0x343fd + 0x269ec3 (no 0x7fff mask) and takes (s &gt;&gt; 16) % count + first, then the first car from there, wrapping inside the span, whose count (+0x34) is below its seats (+0x44) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b750 station window | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:249` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b750 station window] f8 = (1 − k)·A + k·(A + B)/2 and fc = (1 − k)·B + k·(A + B)/2 with k = definition +0x31c (fEntranceSizeInterval, copied unconditionally) and A, B the sample coordinates of the first and third station node ([[node+0x90]+4]+8) |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b7e0 cars in window | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:637` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b7e0 cars in window] car +0x40 + front extent ≥ ctl+0xf8 and car +0x40 − rear extent ≤ ctl+0xfc; OpenTPW's cars have no extents, so a car is inside when its point is |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b8b0 coaster station | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:590` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b8b0 coaster station] in the station when train +0x20 ≥ ctl+0xf8 and train +0x24 ≤ ctl+0xfc; arrival (flag 0x20 clear): 0x43b480(…, 1) unloads the lap's riders; state 4: 0x43b7e0 finds the cars inside the window, the span grows from car 0 to the last car seen, 0x43b600 seats the ring's guests; leaving (flag 0x20 set, out of the window): state 4 seats the free seats of every car from train +0x18, the two lists swap, +0x18/+0x1c = 0, flag 0x20 clears |
| TPW-EXE:0x43b8b0 coaster station | `source/OpenTPW/World/Coasters/CoasterRide.cs:712` | [BIN:TPW-EXE:0x43b8b0 coaster station] the station step is skipped entirely in mode 64 (breakdown); an arriving train unloads open or closed and boarding needs state 4; the tick also skips the station step (and 0x439570) for a train whose flag bit 0x2 is clear (0x435ce5 test byte [esi],2; the train flag writers of bit 0x2 are not identified, the stride-0x60 car flags at 0x43d1ee/0x43d23e are another word), here every train counts as having it set |
